# UKRAINE AND BELARUS — MUSEUMS, BASE COLLECTIONS AND MONUMENT AIRCRAFT

Ukraine held only 3 site records in airplane.museum before this file; Belarus held zero.
Research languages: Ukrainian, Russian, Belarusian. Evidence current to September 2026
unless a row says otherwise. `region` is the literal string `Europe` on every site.

**WAR STATUS IS THE CENTRAL PROBLEM IN THIS FILE AND IS TREATED AS A FIRST-CLASS FIELD.**
Ukraine has been under full-scale invasion since 24 February 2022. Museums have closed,
evacuated, been shelled, or fallen behind the occupation line. Every Ukrainian site below
carries an explicit statement of what is known about its status and the date of the most
recent evidence. Where a site sits in occupied or contested territory that is stated plainly
in `source` and `confidence`, and `confidence` is `low`. Nothing in this file should be read
as an assertion that a pre-2022 listing is still true on the ground.

**A note on `operator_country` for Soviet-legacy airframes.** The rule applied throughout is
stated once here and not repeated on every row: an airframe of a type that was WITHDRAWN FROM
SOVIET SERVICE BEFORE 1991 and plinthed in the Ukrainian or Belarusian SSR never served the
successor state, and is coded `RU` (Soviet Air Force / Soviet Naval Aviation / Aeroflot — the
Russian Federation is the recognised continuator of the USSR and `SU` is not an ISO 3166-1
code). Types still in first-line service in December 1991 were inherited and flown by the
Ukrainian or Belarusian armed forces and are coded `UA` or `BY`. Where a specific airframe's
history is documented the documented history wins over the rule. See NOTES for the full
reasoning and for how to bulk-revise this if the database owner prefers another convention.

## SITES

name: Державний музей авіації України імені О.К. Антонова -- Київ (Жуляни)
city: Київ
state_province: Київ
country: Ukraine
postal_code: 03048
region: Europe
address: вул. Медова 1; Солом'янський район
website: https://aviamuseum.com.ua/
access_type: public
latitude: 50.40609
longitude: 30.46004
source: uk.wikipedia „Державний музей авіації України“ (full published inventory in two tables — aircraft and helicopters — giving registration/tactical number AND construction number for most airframes; last substantive edit 30 December 2025); the museum's own site aviamuseum.com.ua/ua/exposition read directly on 10 September 2026; OpenStreetMap — 87 individually mapped airframe nodes inside the compound (node/7099652974-7099652984; node/7099666185-7099666225; node/7099821533-7099821561; node/7953529344-7953529351; node/13269491113-13269491114)
confidence: HIGH on the collection and its identities — this is the best-documented aviation collection in either country, and the Wikipedia inventory carries construction numbers that make the airframes individually traceable. MEDIUM-LOW on visitor access RIGHT NOW and that is the important caveat. The museum's own website (read 10 September 2026; footer © 2003-2026) states: "Тимчасово працюємо на вул. Любомира Гузара 1. 11 КОРПУС. Ангар КАІ. Медова, 1 не працює" — the institution is temporarily operating from Building 11 hangar on the Kyiv Aviation Institute campus at Liubomyra Huzara 1; THE MEDOVA 1 OPEN-AIR SITE IS NOT OPEN. Advertised hours Tue-Sun 10:00-18:00 (ticket office to 17:00) apply to the temporary venue. The site was closed to visitors from 24 February 2022; uk.wikipedia records that as of 2024 it worked "в закритому режимі" while selling tickets valid after reopening. access_type is recorded `public` because that is the institution's normal and current trading mode — but a visitor turning up at Medova 1 in September 2026 will not get in. The airframes are believed to be in place at Medova 1; I have NO 2026 photographic confirmation of that and cannot exclude damage or relocation. The museum is now a unit of Державний університет «Київський авіаційний інститут» (the former National Aviation University).

Любомира Гузара 1. 11 КОРПУС. Ангар КАІ" as its temporary working venue; uk.wikipedia navbox „Музеї авіації та космонавтики України“ lists „Музей НАУ — Музей історії повітроплавання та літакобудування в Україні“ as a distinct institution
confidence: MEDIUM. The campus airframes are mapped and the campus museum is a real institution named in the Ukrainian Wikipedia navbox, but the uk.wikipedia article „Музей НАУ“ is a 33-byte stub with no content, so the collection is undocumented in text. What raises this site's importance sharply is that the State Aviation Museum's own website names this campus hangar as where it is currently working — so as of September 2026 this is the address at which the public can actually see a State Aviation Museum exhibition. Coordinate is the midpoint of the two mapped airframes. The relationship between the campus museum and the temporary State Aviation Museum exhibition needs a human to untangle.

name: Музей важкої бомбардувальної авіації -- Полтава

address: вул. Олександра Засядька 1; Авіамістечко; Київський район
website: http://mvba.com.ua
access_type: public
latitude: 49.61809
longitude: 34.50233
source: uk.wikipedia „Музей важкої бомбардувальної авіації“ (sortable exhibit table giving bort number AND construction number for eleven airframes, plus former operating regiment for six of them; last substantive edit 31 October 2025); OpenStreetMap — 16 mapped airframe/weapon nodes on the site including the museum polygon named „Музей важкої бомбардувальної авіації“ (node/7085745631-7085745640; node/7109202541-7109202545; node/7974727804-7974727807)
confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH on the collection, LOW-MEDIUM on current access. The published inventory with construction numbers is unusually good and the OSM survey adds four fighters (MiG-21SM, MiG-23MLD, MiG-23UB, MiG-27K) that the Wikipedia table omits. This is a branch of the Національний військово-історичний музей України standing INSIDE the Poltava air base complex (Авіамістечко, the former Poltava-4 closed town). Poltava air base has been struck repeatedly since 2022 and the city suffered a mass-casualty missile strike on 3 September 2024. The uk.wikipedia article records that 3D models of the museum's Tu-95MS and Tu-22M3 were used to train the guidance AI for the Ukrainian Operation Spiderweb strikes of 1 June 2025 — which confirms the airframes were still on site and being surveyed in 2025, the most recent hard evidence I have. Published pre-war hours 10:00-17:00 daily except Monday with a 120 UAH adult ticket; I could NOT confirm those apply in 2026 and wartime access to a live air base's perimeter should be assumed to be at best by prior arrangement.

website:

longitude: 28.50091
source: uk.wikipedia „Музей Повітряних сил Збройних сил України“ (typed exhibit tables for aircraft; helicopters; UAVs; air-to-air and air-to-surface missiles; bombs; SAM systems and radars; cites Воєнно-історичний вісник 38(4) 2020 pp. 231-237 by the museum's director V. O. Alieksieiev); OpenStreetMap — 23 mapped exhibit nodes (node/7074336834-7074336871)
confidence: MEDIUM on the collection, LOW on access. The inventory is well sourced and the OSM survey matches it almost item for item — including a Soyuz-T spacecraft that the Wikipedia aircraft tables do not list. access_type is `restricted` and not `public` because the museum stands on the grounds of the headquarters of the Air Force Command of the Ukrainian Armed Forces: pre-war entry was by prior WRITTEN application only, Monday-Friday 10:00-17:00, with free open-door days on public holidays. The uk.wikipedia article carries the bare sentence "На данний момент не працює" — at present it does not operate. Vinnytsia Air Force Command HQ is a declared and repeatedly struck Russian target; the collection's physical condition in 2026 is unknown to me. Most recent evidence: the 2020 tourist profile of Vinnytsia (41 exhibits; 2800 catalogued items) plus undated post-2022 wiki edits. Six years old on the substance.

name: Музей української авіації -- Луганськ

website:

latitude: 48.52744
longitude: 39.38122
source: uk.wikipedia „Авіаційно-технічний музей (Луганськ)“ (narrative description; former official site avia-museum.lg.ua now dead and reachable only via web.archive.org captures of 2012); OpenStreetMap — 52 individually mapped and individually described airframe nodes with the OSM site polygon named „Музей української авіації“ / „Museum of Ukrainian Aviation“ (node/7058206789-7058206843)
confidence: LOW, and the reason is not the evidence quality but the political and physical situation. THE SITE IS IN RUSSIAN-OCCUPIED TERRITORY AND HAS BEEN SINCE THE SUMMER OF 2014. This was one of the largest aviation collections in Ukraine — 52 mapped airframes, built up from 1996 next to the Luhansk aircraft repair plant on the Hostra Mohyla ridge, absorbing among other things the four helicopters withdrawn from the disbanded 72nd training air base at Konotop in 2005. The OSM node survey is detailed and internally consistent and I have used it as the inventory of record. I have NO evidence of the collection's condition, ownership, naming or continued existence after 2014, let alone after 2022 — Hostra Mohyla is high ground overlooking Luhansk and saw fighting in 2014. Ukrainian sources describe the museum in the past tense. It is recorded here because 52 airframes do not evaporate and researchers need the record; it must NOT be presented to a user as a visitable museum. See NOTES for the reasoning on keeping occupied-territory sites in the database.

source: OpenStreetMap — museum POI at 47.9343/33.3201 named „Музей історії авіації при Криворізькому коледжі НАУ“ plus 13 mapped airframe nodes (node/7053991637-7053991649) and the adjacent „Учбовий полігон КРАУСС“ training-ground polygon; en.wikipedia „Tupolev Tu-114“ Aircraft on display section — "CCCP-76485 – On static display at the Aviation Museum of the National Aviation University in Kryvyi Rih"; uk.wikipedia „Криворізький коледж Державного університету «Київський авіаційний інститут»“ (history of the training aircraft base: Il-12; Il-14; An-2; Tu-104; Mi-4; Mi-8; Il-18; An-10; An-24 in the 1950s-60s then Yak-40; An-24; Tu-134; Tu-114; An-12 and helicopters in the 1970s-80s; and the note that a Tu-154B landed on the grass strip at Zmychka under its own power on 5 October 1979 — the first landing of the type on unpaved ground anywhere)
confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH on the collection and HIGH on the Tu-114, which is independently confirmed by en.wikipedia's survivor list with its registration. This is a training aircraft park that has become a museum, on the grounds of an aviation college; entry is through a college gate so `appointment` rather than `public`. Kryvyi Rih is roughly 60 km from the Kherson-Zaporizhzhia front line and has been struck by ballistic missiles and Shahed drones repeatedly; I have no 2025-26 confirmation that all thirteen airframes survive. OSM node survey dates from 2020.

source: OpenStreetMap — 13 mapped airframe nodes on Aerodromna Street with Wikidata type links (node/7119928549-7119928561)
confidence: LOW. The airframes are mapped with types and Wikidata identifiers, so the inventory is reasonably solid as of the 2020 survey; the OWNING INSTITUTION IS NOT CONFIRMED. The location — Aerodromna Street beside the Sloviansk airfield — and the mix (three An-2, An-24, An-24RV, An-12B, four Mi-8, Yak-40, Yak-52, an L-13 Blaník glider) is the classic signature of an aviation college's training airframe park rather than a museum, and Sloviansk hosts a branch college of the National Aviation University; I did NOT find a source naming the operator and have therefore named the site descriptively rather than asserting the college. Sloviansk has been within roughly 20 km of the front line since 2022, is under regular artillery and missile fire, and was partially evacuated. Assume nothing about the current state of these airframes. access_type `restricted` reflects both a controlled institutional gate and the fact that the area is a military-restricted zone.

name: Національний аерокосмічний університет «ХАІ» — авіаційний майданчик -- Харків

source: OpenStreetMap — university polygon „Національний аерокосмічний університет «Харківський Авіаційний Інститут»“ at вул. Вадима Манька 17 with its named teaching blocks including „Літаковий корпус“ (Aircraft Building), and 10 mapped airframe nodes across the campus (node/7119722840-7119722849; node/8776529384; node/4358227589)
confidence: MEDIUM. The institution is unambiguous — KhAI is one of the two great aviation engineering schools of the former USSR and its campus airframe collection is well known. The individual airframes are mapped with Wikidata type links but with no serials, and the 2020 survey is the most recent evidence. Kharkiv is 30 km from the Russian border and has been under sustained missile, glide-bomb and drone attack since February 2022, with direct strikes on university buildings; KhAI itself has taken damage. Several of these airframes may no longer exist. access_type `appointment` — a working university campus with a gate, not a walk-up museum. Note the street was renamed from вул. Чкалова to вул. Вадима Манька in the 2023-24 decolonisation renaming; older sources give Chkalova 17.

confidence: MEDIUM. The museum POI and the airframe line are both mapped and mutually corroborating, and the adjacent „Чернігівський ліцей з посиленою військово-фізичною підготовкою“ and „ДНВЦ ЗСУ“ polygons place this on the grounds of the former Chernihiv Higher Military Aviation School of Pilots (ЧВВАУЛ) — the school Ukraine's air force fighter pilots trained at until 1995. The airframe line (MiG-15UTI; MiG-17; MiG-21PF x2; MiG-21U; MiG-23M; MiG-23UB; L-29; L-39) is exactly a flying school's type progression. Chernihiv was besieged and heavily shelled between February and April 2022 and remains within Russian artillery-drone reach; a Russian strike on the city centre on 19 August 2023 killed seven. No 2025-26 status confirmation. `public` because the memorial line stands in an open street-side setting; the indoor museum's hours are unknown to me.

A genuine mapped open-air aviation museum on the M-03 highway west of Kharkiv with a coherent nine-airframe collection (An-2; Mi-1; Mi-2; Mi-8; MiG-21; MiG-23; MiG-27; Su-17; Su-27), but it appears in NO published directory I could reach and the only evidence is the 2020 OSM survey. Pisochyn is a Kharkiv satellite settlement and has been within drone and missile range throughout the war. Needs a human to confirm this exists, who runs it and what it is really called.

source: uk.wikipedia „Конотопський музей авіації“ (narrative exhibit list of six Mil helicopters plus a memorial L-39, and the museum's provenance — four helicopters transferred from the Ministry of Defence to the Lazarevskyi local history museum's holdings in 2008, with the Mi-24A donated by the Aviakon repair plant; cites the museum's own 2013 publication); OpenStreetMap — 8 mapped airframe nodes (node/7108828441-7108828448)
confidence: MEDIUM. Good documentary basis and the OSM survey matches the article type for type, adding a node simply labelled „Гвинтокрил“. A department of the Konotop city local history museum; published hours daily 08:00-17:00 closed Sunday and Monday; cockpit access was offered. A restored Wehrmacht bunker on the site opened as an extra exhibit on 7 September 2019. Konotop is in Sumy oblast, 80 km from the Russian border, and has been struck repeatedly — the city was left without power by a strike in 2024 and the Aviakon plant across the road is a declared Russian target. Most recent hard evidence 2019. Assume nothing about 2026.

confidence: MEDIUM. Institution, address and website are all mapped and mutually consistent, and the collection is exactly what a Kharkiv aircraft factory museum should hold — the two airliners Kharkiv built in series (Tu-104 and Tu-124), a Tu-134, a MiG-15UTI, a Yak-18A and a Tu-141 (also Kharkiv-built). `appointment` because it is a works museum inside a defence-industrial enterprise's perimeter. KhSAPE is a declared Russian target and Kharkiv has been under sustained bombardment; the plant was struck in 2022 and again subsequently. Most recent evidence is the 2020 OSM survey. Needs a human to check whether the museum and the airframes still exist.

name: Виставка бойової техніки та озброєння Другої світової війни -- Київ (Печерськ)

website: https://www.warmuseum.kyiv.ua/
access_type: public
latitude: 50.42857
longitude: 30.56097
source: OpenStreetMap — outdoor exhibition polygon named „Виставка бойової техніки та озброєння Другої світової війни“ with 6 mapped airframe nodes inside it (node/6779045199-6779045202; node/7104541114; node/7104541122)
confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH on existence and access, MEDIUM on the airframes. This is the open-air hardware park of the National Museum of the History of Ukraine in the Second World War, the museum under the Motherland Monument on the Dnipro bluff — one of the most visited sites in Kyiv, reopened after 2022 and operating. The Yak-9 is explicitly tagged in OSM as a replica ("макет"). No serials are recorded for any of the six. The museum has been reworking its Soviet-era framing since 2023 (the Soviet coat of arms on the Motherland Monument was replaced with the Ukrainian trident in August 2023) and some hardware has been re-labelled or moved; the outdoor park itself remains open.

confidence: MEDIUM. The L-410 monument is precisely dated and inscribed, which anchors the site; the other five airframes (Il-28; Il-76; An-24RV; MiG-21; Yak-40) are spread across roughly 800 m of the academy campus and are training aids and gate monuments rather than a curated museum. The Flight Academy is Ukraine's civil pilot school. `appointment` — institutional campus. Kropyvnytskyi is a rear-area city but hosts military aviation infrastructure and has been struck. 2020 OSM survey.

Сікорського“, with 4 mapped airframe nodes in the surrounding park (node/1483263078; node/3215404482; node/8823425443; way/939476678) and a dense ring of KPI alumni memorials — Sikorsky; Korolov; Cheloмey; Mikulin; Lyulka; Lyulyev; Kalinin — around them; way/939476678 carries an inscription quoting Antonov on the An-2
confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH on the site, MEDIUM on the airframes. The State Polytechnic Museum and its Sikorsky aviation and cosmonautics department are a real, publicly open Kyiv institution on the KPI campus with a live website; the four airframes stand in the open campus park alongside the alumni bust alley and the „Київським політехнікам — першим підкорювачам неба“ memorial, so they are freely walk-up visible even when the indoor museum is shut. No serials recorded for any of the four. Kyiv is subject to regular missile and drone attack but the campus and museum operate.

source: OpenStreetMap — 4 mapped airframe nodes (node/1640522109; node/1640522115; node/1640522117; node/3617267524) on проспект Повітряних Сил roughly 250 m from the „Національний університет оборони України“ campus polygon at 50.4327/30.4647
confidence: LOW-MEDIUM. Four airframes (MiG-23BN; MiG-23S; MiG-29; Mi-24D) are mapped along the avenue in Kyiv's historic Aviation Settlement, in the block that also contains the National Defence University of Ukraine, the Main Military Prosecutor's Office and the Higher Specialised Court. The most likely host is the National Defence University's display, but I found NO source naming an owner and have therefore named the site by its street rather than asserting an institution. The avenue was renamed from Повітрофлотський проспект in 2023 — older sources use the old name. `public` because the airframes are mapped on the street frontage; if they are in fact inside the Defence University's fence this should become `restricted`. Needs a human with a photograph.

Eight airframes (MiG-21PF; MiG-29; Su-25; Su-27; Mi-2; Mi-6; Mi-8; a Tu-143 reconnaissance drone) in a line on a street whose 2022-24 decolonisation name means "Street of the Guardians of the Sky" — a strong but circumstantial indication of an air force training establishment. The Ivan Kozhedub National University of the Air Force's mapped military landuse polygon is 1.6 km south-east at 50.0225/36.2257, so this is NOT that campus and I will not assert it is. No owning institution confirmed; named by street. `restricted` on the assumption that a coherent eight-airframe military line is behind a fence. Kharkiv front-line city; 2020 survey; treat as unverified.

source: OpenStreetMap — university polygon „База практичного навчання Національного університету цивільного захисту України (НУЦЗУ)“ and „Тренувальний майданчик пожежних“ at 49.9623/36.1905, with 7 mapped airframe nodes spread over roughly 600 m (node/7117126275-7117126281)
confidence: LOW, AND THIS SITE IS FLAGGED AS POSSIBLY NOT A DISPLAY AT ALL. Seven airframes (L-39; An-26; two Mi-2; Mi-24; Su-24M; Tu-141) sit on and around the practical training base of Ukraine's National University of Civil Protection, next to a mapped firefighters' training ground. A rescue service's training ground uses airframes as crash-and-rescue props, and props under repeated foam and cutting practice are not deliberate retention plus public presentation — the contract's test for a display. Against that: a Su-24M and a Tu-141 are strange choices for fire practice, and the site is the former grounds of a Soviet military aviation establishment, so these may be inherited monuments. I have recorded the site rather than silently dropping it, with the doubt stated. A human with one photograph could settle it. Also note there is a „Музей води“ (Water Museum) POI on the same block which is a separate institution and NOT an aviation site.

The tight spacing and the type mix say deliberate memorial group rather than scattered monuments. No inscription, owner or erection date mapped. Mykolaiv was besieged and shelled continuously through 2022 and remains within range; the regional administration building was destroyed by a missile in March 2022. No post-2020 evidence. `public` — street-side memorial.

source: uk.wikipedia „Музей техніки Богуслаєва“ (opened 20 October 2012 in the former young technicians' station building in Klimov Park; itemised outdoor aviation list — An-2; Mi-24V; MiG-15; Yak-18T; Kh-55 cruise missile — plus twenty-one indoor aero-engines from M-11 and M-22 through AI-20; AI-25; D-36 and D-18T, a motorcycle collection, artillery and agricultural tractors; the article carries maintenance banners for lacking sources since March 2021)
confidence: LOW. The museum is real and its aviation content is itemised, but the article is flagged as unsourced, no coordinate is published in it and I did not find the airframes in the OSM sweep — so I have deliberately left latitude and longitude BLANK rather than guess a park centroid. More seriously: the museum is the private creation of Viacheslav Bohuslaiev, the long-time head of Motor Sich, who was arrested in October 2022 on charges of collaboration and aiding the Russian Federation, and Motor Sich was nationalised in November 2022. Zaporizhzhia is a front-line city roughly 30 km from the line of contact. The museum's ownership, name and survival are all open questions. Do not present as visitable.

source: uk.wikipedia „Національний музей космонавтики імені Сергія Павловича Корольова“ (national status conferred by Presidential Decree 380/2020 of 8 September 2020; describes the outdoor rockets beside the „Космос“ pavilion — a geophysical R-5 (8A62) and an R-12 (8K63) — and an indoor collection including the GENUINE Soyuz-27 descent module with its couch and launch key, a Vostok-1 descent module mockup, Luna-2 pennant copy and Luna-3 / Luna-9 mockups, Lunokhod-2 components, and Zenit-2 / Zenit-3SL 1:100 models)
confidence: MEDIUM on the collection, MEDIUM-LOW on 2026 access. A national museum with a live website and an unambiguous, itemised spaceflight collection; the Soyuz-27 descent module is explicitly described as genuine ("справжній") rather than a mockup. No coordinate is published in the article and I did not verify one independently, so latitude and longitude are deliberately BLANK. Zhytomyr was struck by cruise missiles in March 2022 with civilian deaths and remains on the western missile corridor. The museum consists of the Korolov memorial house-museum plus the separate Kosmos exhibition building. Restoration tendering for the building was announced in September 2021; outcome unknown.

confidence: MEDIUM on the site, LOW on any individual missile identity. Coordinate is the {{coord}} value published in the article infobox (48°11'11"N 30°39'52"E). This is Ukraine's nuclear-disarmament museum and its subject matter is the reason the missile_rocket records below exist at all. Access has always been by guided tour with prior arrangement — visitors are taken 33 m underground into the command capsule — hence `appointment`. Pervomaisk is in rear-area Mykolaiv oblast; I found no evidence of war damage but also no 2025-26 confirmation of operation. The site's own domain rvsp.net.ua is dead and reachable only through web.archive.org captures of 2015.

### SITES IN OCCUPIED OR ANNEXED TERRITORY OF UKRAINE
Every site in this group is inside territory under Russian military occupation. `country`
remains `Ukraine` because that is the internationally recognised sovereign state, `confidence`
is `low` on every row without exception, and none of them may be presented to a user as
visitable. They are recorded because the airframes physically exist and a researcher who does
not find them here will waste a season rediscovering them. See NOTES for the reasoning.

confidence: low. IN ANNEXED CRIMEA, at the Donuzlav naval aviation base which the Russian Navy now operates. Both airframes carry dated Russian-language dedications erected UNDER OCCUPATION — the Be-12 marks the centenary of the 318th Independent Anti-Submarine Air Regiment on 27 April 2018, the Ka-27PL honours the 78th Independent Shipborne Anti-Submarine Helicopter Regiment. The Ka-27's `ref` tag RF-57357 is a RUSSIAN Federation military registration, which is consistent with post-2014 Russian Navy use of these units' heritage. These are the clearest case in this file of monuments whose current operator is unambiguously Russian.

Survival not established. No serial.

## SITES — BELARUS

name: Музей авіації і космонавтики Мінського аэраклуба ДТСААФ імя С. І. Грыцаўца -- Мінск (Ліпкі)
city: Мінск
state_province: Мінская вобласць
country: Belarus
postal_code: 
region: Europe
address: Мінскі аэраклуб ДТСААФ імя двойчы Героя Савецкага Саюза С. І. Грыцаўца
website: https://aeroclub-minsk.by/
access_type: public
latitude: 53.90752
longitude: 27.70272
source: The aeroclub's own site aeroclub-minsk.by/vistavochnii_kompleks read directly on 10 September 2026 — "НА ТЕРРИТОРИИ АЭРОКЛУБА РАБОТАЕТ ВЫСТАВОЧНЫЙ КОМПЛЕКС АВИАЦИОННОЙ ТЕХНИКИ И ПАВИЛЬОН «КОСМОС»"; hours Tuesday-Sunday 10:00-18:00 closed Monday; a new Minsktrans bus route No. 262 runs from Avtozavodskaya bus station to the stop „Музей авиации и космонавтики“ Tuesday-Sunday from 1 May; ru.wikipedia „Музей авиационной техники Центрального аэроклуба ДОСААФ имени С. Грицевца“ (opened 4 July 2009 with 26 exhibits; re-established 19 October 2024 after the move; full type list); be.wikipedia „Музей авіяцыйнай тэхнікі“ (officially CLOSED 20 October 2023 pending relocation, citing aircraft-museum.ucoz.ru of that date); OpenStreetMap museum POI „Музей авиации и космонавтики“ at 53.9075/27.7027
confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH and CURRENT — this is the only site in the whole file whose operating status I could confirm from a live first-party source in September 2026. THE COORDINATE MATTERS AND THE PUBLISHED ONES ARE WRONG. ru.wikipedia's infobox still gives 53°57'38.4"N 27°39'6.9"E, which reverse-geocodes to Pilotna Street in the „Новая Боровая“ housing development in Kopishcha — the Borovaya airfield land, which was sold for redevelopment and is exactly why the museum had to move. The museum closed at Borovaya on 20 October 2023 and reopened on 19 October 2024 beside the DOSAAF aeroclub at Lipki. The coordinate recorded here is the OSM museum POI at Lipki. ru.wikipedia's street address „ул. Липковская 12А“ is also doubtful: Lipkovskaya Street is in Stsiapianka 2 km north-east and is not the aeroclub. Address is therefore recorded by institution rather than by street. Belarus is not at war and this museum is genuinely open — but it is a DOSAAF (state paramilitary sport society) facility, so expect a gate and a ticket desk rather than a municipal museum.

confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH on the identities, MEDIUM on the presentation. This is the single best-documented set of airframe identities in Belarus: the OSM surveyor recorded each aircraft as type + registration + operator — Tu-154B-2 EW-85581 Belavia, Il-76MD EW-76709 TransAviaExport, An-24RV EW-47291 GomelAvia, Tu-134A CCCP-65036 Aeroflot, An-2 EW-237CD Grodno airline, Yak-40 EW-88202 MinskAvia. A retired-airliner line at Minsk National Airport, on the terminal side. `public` because airport landside display areas are normally walk-up; if in fact airside this should become `restricted`. No survey date newer than the OSM node history.

name: Лі-2 Беларускага дзяржаўнага музея гісторыі Вялікай Айчыннай вайны -- Мінск

longitude: 27.53707
source: OpenStreetMap way/384806738 („Лі-2“) immediately beside the Belarusian State Museum of the History of the Great Patriotic War on praspiekt Peramožcaŭ; ru.wikipedia „Музей авиационной техники…“ notes that the aviation museum's Li-2 (a 1953 airframe) came FROM the Great Patriotic War Museum's exhibition
confidence: MEDIUM, WITH A CONFLICT STATED. The Li-2 airframe is currently mapped at the Great Patriotic War Museum's new building on praspiekt Peramožcaŭ, yet the Russian Wikipedia article on the aviation museum says a Li-2 whose previous location was the Great Patriotic War Museum went to Borovaya in 2009. Either these are two different Li-2s, or one of the two sources is out of date, or the airframe went to Borovaya and came back. I have recorded it here at the mapped position and flagged the conflict rather than silently choosing. The museum itself is a flagship national institution, fully open, ticketed, walk-up.

Machulishchy is the 61st Fighter Air Base of the Belarusian Air Force and the airfield where Russian A-50 AEW aircraft were based and where one was damaged by a partisan drone attack on 26 February 2023. Two airframes are mapped in the base's heritage area. The OSM nodes give NO type, so both airframes are recorded as Unidentified rather than guessed. `restricted` — this is inside an active military air base. This site is named in the research brief as a target and I confirm it exists in OSM, but I could establish nothing about what is actually on the plinths. Needs a human.

Recorded because a described open-air aviation museum is exactly the kind of site that otherwise never enters a database. Needs a human.

source: OpenStreetMap way/241248409 — name „Помнік авіяканструктару П. Сухому: самалёт Су-17М3“, ref 101, inscription „Авиаконструктору Павлу Сухому от земляков — комплекс банка Барановичского авиаремонтного…“
confidence: MEDIUM — the best-documented monument in Belarus outside the Minsk museum. Pavel Sukhoi was born in Hlybokaye district and the Su-17M3 was erected by his fellow-countrymen with funding named in the inscription, which credits the Baranavichy aircraft repair plant among the contributors. The bort number 101 is recorded in the OSM `ref` tag and is carried into `tail_number` below. Two further Sukhoi airframes are mapped within 2.5 km (see the next two sites) — Hlybokaye appears to have made the designer a local theme.

source: OpenStreetMap node/1483751824 — name „Лётчыкам 33-га знішчальнага палка“, description „Самолёт МиГ-15“
confidence: MEDIUM on dedication, low on the airframe. The monument honours the pilots of the 33rd Fighter Aviation Regiment, which was based at Pruzhany and was among the units destroyed on the ground on 22 June 1941. The AIRFRAME IS A MiG-15, a type that did not exist in 1941 — a very common Soviet substitution where a jet stands in for the piston fighters it commemorates. That is recorded in the description rather than allowed to corrupt the identity. No serial.

name: Помнік лётчыкам бамбардзіровачных палкоў -- Бяроза (Асаўцы)
city: Бяроза
state_province: Брэсцкая вобласць

website: 
access_type: public
latitude: 52.54290
longitude: 24.99589
source: OpenStreetMap node/4184340020 (МіГ-21; inscription „В городе Берёза на аэродроме «Осовцы» с 1955 по 2010 г базировался Кёнигсбергский Краснознамённый…“) and node/4184340022 (МІГ-17П; inscription „Героическим лётчикам 97 121 125 130 162 207 212 бомбардировочных авиаполков храбро сражавшимся…“)
confidence: MEDIUM. Two inscribed monuments about 1.9 km apart at the Byaroza / Osovtsy airfield. The MiG-21's inscription dates the resident regiment's occupation of the field from 1955 to 2010, which is why the MiG-21 is coded BY — Belarus operated MiG-21s well past 1991. The MiG-17P commemorates seven named bomber regiments of the Second World War and is again a later type standing for earlier ones; that is stated in its description. Coordinate is the midpoint of the two.

confidence: MEDIUM. An unusually well-provenanced village monument: the inscription records that the aircraft was PRESENTED TO THE MALECH AGRICULTURAL FIRM BY THE COMMANDER OF THE 26TH AIR ARMY, Lieutenant-General S. V. Sedov. The 26th Air Army was the Soviet air army covering the Belorussian Military District, so this is a Soviet-era gift and the airframe never served independent Belarus — coded RU accordingly. No serial.

The OSM name „МіГ-15ВБЗ“ is not a real MiG-15 sub-type — it is almost certainly a garbled rendering of МиГ-15УТИ or of a boiler-plate „ВВС“ string; the model is therefore recorded as a plain MiG-15 with NO variant guessed and the odd string preserved in aliases. No serial.

confidence: low-MEDIUM. An Il-2 Shturmovik monument beside the Kurhan Slavy (Mound of Glory) memorial complex on the Minsk-Moscow highway, the principal Belarusian memorial to Operation Bagration. Genuine Il-2 airframes are extremely rare and most Il-2 monuments in the former USSR are full-scale replicas or recovered-and-rebuilt wrecks — I could NOT establish which this is and have flagged it rather than asserting it is original. No serial.

name: Самалёт PWS-26 -- Брэсцкая крэпасць
city: Брэст
state_province: Брэсцкая вобласць
country: Belarus
postal_code: 
region: Europe
address: Брэсцкая крэпасць; Брэст
website: 
access_type: public
latitude: 52.02323
longitude: 23.12048
source: OpenStreetMap node — „Samolot PWS-26 81-71“ at 52.02323/23.12048 inside the Brest Fortress complex
confidence: low-MEDIUM, AND FLAGGED AS ALMOST CERTAINLY A REPLICA. A PWS-26 — the Polish Podlasie works' 1930s biplane trainer — displayed at Brest Fortress, which was Polish Brześć until September 1939. The OSM name carries the marking 81-71. NO GENUINE PWS-26 IS KNOWN TO SURVIVE ANYWHERE, so this is a replica or a reproduction unless proven otherwise, and 81-71 should be treated as a painted marking rather than an identity. Recorded here because the contract counts flagged replicas. Needs a human with a photograph of the placard.

### Пам'ятник Ту-16П -- Прилуки

Tupolev|Tu-16|P||||fixed_wing|monoplane|military|electronic_warfare||OpenStreetMap node/5213295689. Pryluky was the base of the 184th Heavy Bomber Aviation Regiment which flew Tu-16 and later Tu-160; the monument stands on вулиця Авіаторів in the closed military settlement. The Tu-16 left Soviet service around the collapse of the USSR and Ukraine flew the type only briefly. No serial; construction number or erection date recorded|Tu-16;Tu16;Tu-16P;Badger|Пам'ятник Ту-16П -- Прилуки|on_display|RU

### Пам'ятник Ту-95У -- Узин

Tupolev|Tu-95|U||||fixed_wing|monoplane|military|trainer||OpenStreetMap node/7099622954. Uzyn was the base of the 1006th Heavy Bomber Aviation Regiment; the U suffix marks a training conversion. Ukraine inherited Tu-95s at Uzyn in 1992 and scrapped or returned them under the trilateral disarmament agreements — this airframe is the survivor. No serial; construction number or erection date recorded|Tu-95;Tu95;Tu-95U;Bear|Пам'ятник Ту-95У -- Узин|on_display|UA

## NOTES

### Was search available

**General web search was NOT usable and was never successfully used in this pass.** The shared
WebSearch quota was exhausted before this agent started, as the brief warned. Everything below
was obtained from APIs and direct fetches:

- **MediaWiki `action=parse` and `action=query&list=search` against uk.wikipedia, ru.wikipedia,
  be.wikipedia and en.wikipedia.** These worked but are HEAVILY RATE LIMITED from this
  environment — the first three requests in any burst return HTTP 429 „You are making too many
  requests to the API“. A throttled fetcher with a descriptive User-Agent, a 6-second minimum
  interval, exponential backoff over six attempts and an on-disk cache got a 100 per cent
  success rate. Anyone repeating this work should build that fetcher FIRST; without it the
  Wikipedia APIs look broken when they are merely impatient.
- **Overpass.** `overpass-api.de` is blocked by the proxy as the brief said. Of the three
  mirrors, **maps.mail.ru/osm/tools/overpass answered every bbox query it was given**;
  `overpass.kumi.systems` and `overpass.private.coffee` answered some and returned nginx
  504 Gateway Time-out on others. **The `area["ISO3166-1"]` form TIMED OUT on all three
  mirrors for both Ukraine and Belarus** — contrary to the brief's report from another agent —
  so this pass used bounding boxes split into quarters and then reverse-geocoded every single
  node to establish its country. That turned out to be the right call anyway (see below).
- **Nominatim** — both `/reverse` and `/search` — worked flawlessly at a 1.15-second interval
  and did more analytical work in this pass than any other tool.
- **Direct WebFetch of known URLs** worked and produced the two most valuable current-status
  findings in the file (aviamuseum.com.ua and aeroclub-minsk.by).

### Sources and their weight

**Ukrainian Wikipedia carried the museums; OpenStreetMap carried everything else.**

`uk.wikipedia`'s article on the **State Aviation Museum** publishes the museum's full inventory
as two sortable tables giving, for most airframes, BOTH the tactical or civil registration AND
the construction number, plus the acquisition year and the previous operator. That single
article is worth more than every English-language aviation directory covering Ukraine combined,
and it is the reason the Kyiv rows in this file carry construction numbers where almost nothing
else in the region does. The **Poltava** article does the same thing for eleven airframes and
adds the former operating regiment for six of them. The **Vinnytsia** article publishes typed
tables down to individual bomb and missile types. English coverage of all three is a fraction
of this. The brief's instruction to search in the local language is the single reason this file
has serials in it at all.

The `uk.wikipedia` navbox **„Музеї авіації та космонавтики України“** is the best lead list for
Ukraine that exists anywhere and should be the starting point for anyone extending this work.
It named Konotop, Luhansk, the NAU museum and the Motor Sich collection, none of which I would
have found otherwise.

**OpenStreetMap produced 397 individually mapped Ukrainian airframes and 61 Belarusian ones.**
Ukrainian OSM surveyors have mapped monument aircraft with a thoroughness I have not seen
described for any other country in this project: individual nodes per airframe, with
`name:uk`, `name:en`, Wikidata type links, and in the Luhansk museum's case a paragraph of
descriptive text on every one of 52 nodes. Roughly 100 of the 163 sites in this file exist in
no published directory at all and were recovered purely from OSM plus a reverse geocode.

**The airframes with the best identities in this whole file are at Minsk National Airport**,
where an OSM surveyor recorded type, full registration and operating airline in a single name
tag for six airliners. That is better data than most museums publish about themselves.

### Corrections made

- **The Kyiv museum's „Ту-95МС“ is a Tu-142M3.** OSM node 7099821557 labels it Tu-95MS; the
  museum's own published inventory lists a Tu-142M3 (bort 85 black, c/n 8601903) and no Tu-95
  at all. The Tu-142 is the naval derivative of the Tu-95 and the two are near-indistinguishable
  from outside a fence. Recorded as Tu-142M3 with the OSM label called out as an error. Poltava
  DOES have a genuine Tu-95MS (bort 01, c/n 32191) and the two must not be conflated.
- **Kyiv MiG-15UTI and MiG-17F are given the same construction number, 612341, by the source.**
  That is impossible. Neither construction number is recorded as fact; both entries say so.
- **Kyiv's second Tu-134A is given construction number 6350003**, which is a Tu-104-format
  number and adjacent to the number the same table gives the museum's own Tu-104 (6350102).
  Almost certainly a transcription slip. Not recorded as fact.
- **Kyiv MiG-25RB is labelled „MiG-25PB“ in OSM's English name tag.** There is no MiG-25PB.
  Corrected to RB per the museum inventory.
- **The Kyiv museum inventory describes the Yak-18PM as „середньомагістральний пасажирський
  літак“ — a medium-haul airliner.** That is a copy-paste fault in the wiki table. Ignored.
- **Poltava's Tu-22: the article's prose says Ту-22КД and its own table says Ту-22КП.** The
  Commons photograph is filed as Tu-22KD. KD recorded, KP kept in aliases.
- **„МіГ-15ВБЗ“ (Vaŭkavysk) and „МіГ-15ВТЗ“ (Slonim) are not real sub-type designations.**
  Both are almost certainly garbled renderings. Recorded as plain MiG-15 with no variant
  guessed and the surveyor's string preserved in aliases.
- **The Minsk aviation museum's published coordinate is wrong and its published address is
  doubtful.** ru.wikipedia still gives 53°57'38.4"N 27°39'6.9"E, which reverse-geocodes to
  Pilotna Street in the „Новая Боровая“ HOUSING DEVELOPMENT — the airfield land the museum was
  evicted from. The museum closed at Borovaya on 20 October 2023 and reopened at Lipki on
  19 October 2024. The coordinate in this file is the OSM museum POI at Lipki (53.90752,
  27.70272). The article's „ул. Липковская 12А“ points at a street 2 km away in Stsiapianka
  and is not used.
- **Zhytomyr's Su-9 monument node carries an OSM description of a ground-attack aircraft**,
  which fits the Su-25, not the Su-9 interceptor. The surveyor pasted the wrong boiler-plate.
  Type taken at face value, mismatch recorded.
- **Three OSM airframes I initially bucketed as Ukrainian are not.** The bbox sweep over
  44.3-52.4 N / 22.1-40.3 E returned 533 elements of which only 397 — 74 per cent — are
  actually in Ukraine: 82 are in Romania, 36 in Russia, 9 in Poland, 5 in Belarus and 4 in
  Moldova. A bbox result for this region is one quarter foreign and MUST be reverse-geocoded
  before anything is assigned a country. Similarly the Belarus sweep pulled in the Lithuanian
  Aviation Museum at Kaunas (54.878, 23.890 — nine airframes) and a node in Latvia; both were
  excluded on the reverse geocode, and the Chernihiv, Snovsk, Horodnia, Koriukivka, Mena and
  Ovruch airframes that the Belarus boxes returned are Ukrainian and are filed as such.

### Judgment calls

**1. Occupied and annexed territory: RECORDED, not omitted.** Seventeen sites in this file are
in Crimea, occupied Luhansk oblast, occupied left-bank Kherson oblast or occupied Enerhodar.
I record them because the airframes physically exist, because a database that quietly drops
them will have someone re-research them every year, and because `country: Ukraine` is the
correct value under international law regardless of who currently holds the ground. Every one
carries `confidence: low`, an explicit statement of occupation in the source and confidence
fields, and — for the Donuzlav memorial — the fact that its Russian-language dedications were
erected under occupation and its Ka-27 wears a Russian Federation registration. **If the
database owner would rather these were suppressed, they are all grouped under one heading and
are trivially removable. That is a policy decision and I have not made it unilaterally in
either direction.**

**2. Vovchansk is recorded and flagged as presumed destroyed.** The DOSAAF aviation centre
monument there is the one row in this file I would bet against surviving: Vovchansk was
subjected to a ground offensive from May 2024 and has been essentially levelled. Recording it
with the destruction stated is more useful than deleting it, because it tells the next
researcher what happened rather than leaving a gap.

**3. `access_type` for the Kyiv State Aviation Museum is `public` and I am not comfortable
with it.** The institution is trading, selling tickets, and running an exhibition Tue-Sun
10:00-18:00 — but at a temporary venue, and Medova 1 itself is shut. None of the three enum
values means "an open museum whose main site is closed". `public` reflects the institution's
mode; the confidence field states the reality in full. Same reasoning as the Belgrade
Aeronautical Museum in the Balkans file.

**4. `operator_country` and the pre-1991 rule.** The brief says code `RU` for airframes that
only ever served the USSR. Applied literally that is right but jarring, so I applied a
defensible mechanical rule and am stating it so it can be reversed in bulk:
*types withdrawn from Soviet service before 1991* — MiG-15, MiG-17, MiG-19, Su-7, Su-9,
Yak-3/9/11/18/28/38, Tu-2, Tu-16, Tu-104, Tu-114, Tu-124, Li-2, Il-2, Il-14, Il-18, Il-28,
Il-38, Mi-1, Mi-4, Ka-25, An-10, An-14, La-17 — get `RU`; *types still in first-line service in
December 1991 and inherited by the successor state* — MiG-21 onwards, Su-15 onwards, Tu-22,
Tu-22M, Tu-95, Tu-134, Tu-154, Tu-141/143, An-2/12/24/26/30, Il-76, Mi-2/6/8/14/24/26,
Ka-26/27, L-29, L-39, L-410 — get `UA` or `BY`. **A documented individual history always beats
the rule** and did so in eight places: the Zelva Su-24MR (116th Air Base until 2010 → BY), the
Postavy Su-24M (inscribed „с 1993 по 1995 г“ → BY), the Byaroza MiG-21 (regiment based there
1955-2010 → BY), the Malech Su-7U (a gift from the Soviet 26th Air Army → RU), the Donuzlav
Ka-27PL (Russian registration RF-57357 → RU), the Minsk airport Tu-134A (Soviet registration
and Aeroflot markings → RU), the Kyiv Yak-38 (ex TAVKR Kyiv, Soviet Naval Aviation, never
Ukrainian → RU) and the Kyiv Be-6 and Be-12 (explicitly „ВМС України“ → UA).
`operator_country` is left BLANK on eleven rows: every replica (both Anasal replicas, the
Wright Flyer, the Kyiv Yak-3, the Kurhan Slavy Il-2 is RU because the type is Soviet
regardless), the Donuzlav Be-12 whose pre-2014 history is unestablished, the Minsk OW-1
experimental aircraft, and the untyped monument airframes at Vovchansk, Desnianka, Tarasivka,
Zatyshne, Zymohiria, Machulishchy, Babruisk, Vitebsk, Siano, Polatsk, Byaroza, Voranava,
Lahoysk, Chachersk, Krychaw, Cherykaw and the three Crimean untagged nodes.

**5. Replicas recorded and flagged.** The two Anatra Anasal replicas (Kyiv Medova 1, built 2004;
Kyiv KAI campus, labelled „Копія … 1916 року“), the 2003 Wright Flyer replica (recorded
`in_storage` because the museum's own inventory says it is not currently exhibited), the
Yak-9 at the Kyiv WWII museum (OSM tags it „макет“), the Brest Fortress PWS-26 (no original
PWS-26 survives anywhere, so 81-71 is a painted marking not an identity), the Zhytomyr Vostok-1
descent module and Luna-9 lander (both explicitly captioned „макет“), and the Pervomaisk RS-22
nose section (a mockup beside the real silo). Four more are flagged as NEEDING a replica check
rather than asserted either way: the Kyiv Yak-3, the Kyiv ANT-7/R-6, the Troieshchyna Tu-2 and
the Kurhan Slavy Il-2 — all four are types of which genuine survivors are vanishingly rare.

**6. Fuselage sections and part-airframes recorded as distinct objects.** The Kyiv museum's
MiG-25 fuselage (OSM node 7099652974) is recorded separately from its complete MiG-25RB, and
its unidentified German aircraft fuselage (node 7099652975) is recorded as `Unidentified` with
`operator_country: DE` — the only thing about it that is certain. The Kharkiv KhAI campus
Yak-40 fuselage is likewise recorded from its own node.

**7. Thirty-one airframes are recorded as `Unidentified`** because their OSM node carries no
type at all, or only the generic word „Самолёт“ / „Верталёт“ / „Су“ / „МІГ“. The contract is
explicit that these must not be dropped and must not be guessed, and I have not guessed a
single one — including the two at Machulishchy, which the brief specifically asked about and
where a guess would have been very tempting.

**8. The Kyiv museum has grown beyond its published inventory and I did not invent the
difference.** OSM maps 87 airframe nodes at Medova 1 against roughly 70 entries in the
uk.wikipedia tables. I added only those OSM-only airframes that are unambiguously a type the
table does not contain: the L-410, the An-24T, the Mi-24RKhR, the Mi-35, the MiG-25 fuselage,
the German fuselage and the Il-28 under restoration. Where OSM shows MORE of a type the table
already has — four Ka-26 nodes against one inventoried Ka-26, three Su-25 against two, three
MiG-29 against two, two Yak-38 against one — I recorded the inventoried airframes with their
serials and stated the count discrepancy in the description rather than emitting anonymous
duplicate rows. **Under-recording is recoverable; inventing airframes is not.**

**9. Two sites are recorded with zero aircraft, deliberately.** The Poltava Museum of Aviation
and Cosmonautics and the Voronovytsia Museum of the History of Aviation and Cosmonautics are
indoor document-and-model museums. A 1:n model of a K-7 is not an airframe. They are recorded
as sites because they are real named aviation museums that a researcher will search for, and
because the Poltava one is the parent institution that assembled the far more important
long-range aviation collection across town.

### EXCLUDED, and why

- **Lithuanian Aviation Museum, Kaunas (54.878, 23.890)** — nine airframes returned by the
  Belarus bounding box. In Lithuania. Not this brief.
- **A node at 55.898, 23.376** — returned by the Belarus box; reverse-geocodes outside Belarus.
- **82 Romanian, 36 Russian, 9 Polish and 4 Moldovan airframes** returned by the Ukraine
  bounding boxes. All excluded on the reverse geocode. The Romanian ones in particular
  cluster around 47.9/28.3 and will contaminate any Ukraine bbox sweep that does not
  reverse-geocode.
- **Baranavichy aviation museum** — the brief asked for it by name. I found a Su-17 MONUMENT at
  Baranavichy (OSM node 7442158443) and the 558th aircraft repair plant, which is named as a
  funding contributor on the Hlybokaye Sukhoi monument — but NO evidence of a museum. Recorded
  the monument; did not invent the museum. A well-searched zero.
- **Lida aviation museum** — same. Found the 206th Assault Aviation Base's Su-25 gate guard
  (bort 206) and recorded it; found no museum.
- **„Вялікі камень“ / Great Stone industrial park** — named in the brief. Nothing aviation-related
  found in OSM, Wikipedia or Nominatim. Zero.
- **Belarusian State Museum of the History of the Great Patriotic War, Minsk** — named in the
  brief. RECORDED, but only for the single Li-2 mapped beside it; the museum's indoor
  collection is documents, weapons and vehicles and I found no other airframe.
- **Kharkiv „Музей води“ (Water Museum)** — sits on the same block as the NUCZU airframes and
  will mislead anyone reading the Overpass output. It is not an aviation site.
- **Kropyvnytskyi „Гуртожиток (ЛА НАУ)“** — a hall of residence polygon, not a museum; used
  only to identify the Flight Academy campus.
- **Kyiv „Державний політехнічний музей“ locomotive, tank and gun memorials** — the KPI campus
  park contains a steam locomotive, a T-34-85 and artillery alongside the four airframes. Only
  the airframes are recorded.
- **The Chernihiv „Нова Пошта“ parcel locker, pharmacy and bank POIs** and their equivalents
  elsewhere — noise from the institution-identification Overpass query, listed here only so
  nobody re-runs that query expecting them to mean something.
- **Active flightlines.** No aircraft at Poltava, Vasylkiv, Myrhorod, Starokostiantyniv, Lida,
  Baranavichy or Machulishchy air bases is recorded except where it is explicitly a plinthed
  monument or inside a museum compound. Operational aircraft are not displays.

### Blank fields, deliberately

- **`latitude` and `longitude` are BLANK on four sites**: Motor Sich Zaporizhzhia, the Zhytomyr
  Korolov cosmonautics museum, and the Voronovytsia museum — because no coordinate is published
  in the source article and I would have had to guess a park or town centroid. Pervomaisk and
  the Poltava aviation and cosmonautics museum DO have coordinates because their articles
  publish a `{{coord}}` value, which is recorded and identified as such. A guessed coordinate
  that is 2 km out is worse than no coordinate.
- **`postal_code` is blank on every Belarusian site except the Great Patriotic War Museum**, and
  on a handful of Ukrainian ones. Nominatim returned no postcode for those localities and I did
  not substitute an oblast-level guess.
- **`website` is blank on every monument site** — monuments do not have websites — and on
  Vinnytsia, whose museum has never had one.
- **`year_built` is blank on 496 of 497 aircraft rows.** The single exception is the Kyiv
  Tu-154 CCCP-85020, whose construction number 71A020 encodes 1971 in the Tu-154 numbering
  scheme; the description says the year is derived from the construction number and nothing
  else. Nowhere else did I have a sourced construction, roll-out, first-flight or delivery
  date, and a bort number is never a year.
- **`tail_number` is blank on roughly four hundred rows.** Soviet-legacy bort numbers are two
  or three digits, repeat endlessly, and are repainted; where OSM recorded one in a `ref` tag
  (Hlybokaye 101, Byahoml 02, Postavy 305, Lida 206, Shchuchyn 18, Zelva 77, Hlybokaye 02) it is
  recorded, and where the Kyiv, Poltava and Minsk-airport inventories publish one it is
  recorded. Everywhere else a blank is the honest answer.

### Needs a human, ranked

1. **Is the State Aviation Museum's collection at Medova 1 intact?** This is the single most
   important open question in the file — roughly 70 to 87 airframes including the only An-71,
   the Il-86 prototype, the Il-18 second prototype, the first production Tu-104 and a Chornobyl
   liquidator Mi-26. The museum is contactable: **(044) 451-83-14** (administration),
   **(067) 284-47-73** (excursions), **info@aviamuseum.com.ua**, https://aviamuseum.com.ua/.
   One call would also settle what is actually on show at the temporary KAI hangar venue at
   Liubomyra Huzara 1 and whether the Il-28 restoration continued after February 2022.
2. **Poltava Museum of Heavy Bomber Aviation — is it open, and to whom?** The only Tu-160 on
   museum display in the world sits inside a struck air base. Site http://mvba.com.ua;
   parent institution the National Military History Museum of Ukraine.
3. **Vinnytsia Museum of the Air Force.** The uk.wikipedia article says flatly „На данний
   момент не працює“. Is that a wartime suspension or a closure, and where are the 41 exhibits?
   Contact is through the Air Force Command; the museum's director as of the article is
   Major-General (ret.) Volodymyr Alieksieiev.
4. **The Minsk Li-2 conflict.** Is the Li-2 at the Great Patriotic War Museum the same airframe
   ru.wikipedia says went to Borovaya in 2009, or are there two? The aeroclub is contactable on
   **+375 44 583 50 95** (excursions) and https://aeroclub-minsk.by/; the war museum at
   https://www.warmuseum.by/.
5. **What is the Minsk museum's „экспериментальный самолёт OW-1“?** A one-off experimental
   aeroplane about which I could establish nothing at all. Same phone number as above. This is
   the most interesting single unknown in Belarus.
6. **Machulishchy — what are the two untyped airframes at the 61st Air Base memorial?** The
   brief asked specifically. One photograph settles it.
7. **The Kharkiv „вулиця Вартових Неба“ eight-airframe line — who owns it?** Almost certainly a
   military aviation training establishment but I will not assert which. Likewise the Sloviansk
   Aerodromna Street thirteen-airframe park (probably the NAU branch college) and the Kyiv
   проспект Повітряних Сил four (probably the National Defence University, +380 44 271 06 30,
   https://nuou.org.ua/).
8. **Are the seven NUCZU Novoselivka airframes displays or rescue-training props?** If props,
   delete the site. National University of Civil Protection of Ukraine, Kharkiv.
9. **Motor Sich / Bohuslaiev Technology Museum, Zaporizhzhia.** Ownership, name and survival all
   unresolved after the 2022 nationalisation and the founder's arrest. Also needs a coordinate.
10. **Confirm the „Музей авіації“ at Pisochyn exists** and find out what it is really called.
    Nine airframes and not a single published reference.
11. **The Konotop „Гвинтокрил“** — an eighth helicopter at a museum whose published list has
    seven aircraft. Konotop Local History Museum named for O. M. Lazarevskyi.
12. **Replica checks**, in order of value: the Kyiv ANT-7/R-6, the Kyiv Yak-3, the Troieshchyna
    Tu-2, the Kurhan Slavy Il-2, the Brest PWS-26. Each is a type whose genuine survivors could
    be counted on one hand, so an original would be internationally significant and a replica
    should be labelled before someone repeats the claim.
